[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"bf8c19","wall_time_seconds":0.8770491,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4551,"output":"{\\r\\n  \\"github_physical_files\\": 1215,\\r\\n  \\"github_bytes\\": 65728676,\\r\\n  \\"x_own_ids\\": 2072,\\r\\n  \\"x_foreign_rows\\": 111,\\r\\n  \\"selected_posts\\": 45\\r\\n}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2040572272944324650\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-04T23:28:36.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Farzapedia, personal wikipedia of Farza, good example following my Wiki LLM tweet.\\\\n\\\\nI really like this approach to personalization in a number of ways, compared to \\\\\\"status quo\\\\\\" of an AI that allegedly gets better the more you use it or something:\\\\n\\\\n1. Explicit. The memory artifact is explicit and navigable (the wiki), you can see exactly what the AI does and does not know and you can inspect and manage this artifact, even if you don\'t do the direct text writing (the LLM does). The knowledge of you is not implicit and unknown, it\'s explicit and viewable.\\\\n2. Yours. Your data is yours, on your local computer, it\'s not in some particular AI provider\'s system without the ability to extract it. You\'re in control of your information. \\\\n3. File over app. The memory here is a simple collection of files in universal formats (images, markdown). This means the data is interoperable: you can use a very large collection of tools/CLIs or whatever you want over this information because it\'s just files. The agents can apply the entire Unix toolkit over them. They can natively read and understand them. Any kind of data can be imported into files as input, and any kind of interface can be used to view them as the output. E.g. you can use Obsidian to view them or vibe code something of your own. Search \\\\\\"File over�app\\\\\\" for an article on this philosophy.\\\\n4. BYOAI. You can use whatever AI you want to \\\\\\"plug into\\\\\\" this information - Claude, Codex, OpenCode, whatever. You can even think about taking an open source AI and finetuning it on your wiki - in principle, this AI could \\\\\\"know\\\\\\" you in its weights, not just attend over your data.\\\\n\\\\nSo this approach to personalization puts *you* in full control. The data is yours. In Universal formats. Explicit and inspectable. Use whatever AI you want over it, keep the AI companies on their toes! :)\\\\n\\\\nCertainly this is not the simplest way to get an AI to know you - it does require you to manage file directories and so on, but agents also make it quite simple and they can help you a lot. I imagine a number of products might come out to make this all easier, but imo \\\\\\"agent proficiency\\\\\\" is a CORE SKILL of the 21st century. These are extremely powerful tools - they speak English and they do all the computer stuff for you. Try this opportunity to play with one.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2040572272944324650\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"25550d76660f40ffa2eca46415b973636f04d6d8a286f25b7a8f20652c10e814\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2040473058834878662\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-04T16:54:22.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Peter Steinberger told me that he wants PR to be \\\\\\"prompt request\\\\\\". His agents are perfectly capable of implementing most ideas, so there is no need to take your idea, expand it into a vibe coded mess using free tier ChatGPT and send that as a PR, which is now most PRs.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2040473058834878662\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"4cad309ec1569c6e5424c17fd96acf07f0734a5c7e29e2eb37b8a4c069b314a8\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2040470801506541998\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-04T16:45:23.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Wow, this tweet went very viral!\\\\n\\\\nI wanted share a possibly slightly improved version of the tweet in an \\\\\\"idea file\\\\\\". The idea of the idea file is that in this era of LLM agents, there is less of a point/need of sharing the specific code/app, you just share the idea, then the other person\'s agent customizes & builds it for your specific needs.\\\\n\\\\nSo here\'s the idea in a gist format: \\\\nhttps://\\\\ngist.github.com/karpathy/442a6\\\\nbf555914893e9891c11519de94f\\\\n�\\\\n\\\\nYou can give this to your agent and it can build you your own LLM wiki and guide you on how to use it etc. It\'s intentionally kept a little bit abstract/vague because there are so many directions to take this in. And ofc, people can adjust the idea or contribute their own in the Discussion which is cool.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2040470801506541998\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"ca390933df863862d0f6ff1342d5bf37342dd91ca121641e1f1b730e8e4e3524\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2039816150062948769\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-02T21:24:02.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Haha I vibe code products with twitter :D\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2039816150062948769\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"817d88741a176733c0de32b2292b928449109a93718ca08d79dc88f5411e7ba9\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2039814066575917263\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-02T21:15:46.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Currently no because I\'m trying to keep it super simple and flat, it\'s just a nested directory of .md files and .png files and a few .csv and .py, and the schema is kept up to date in AGENTS.md . The LLMs get this very easily. Any custom functions are easy to vibe code tools for.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2039814066575917263\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"537d6b5c40ff87fedbde5b5dbbf53a5ed80a7763a216830ef846519bcc541802\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2039805659525644595\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-04-02T20:42:21.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"LLM Knowledge Bases\\\\n\\\\nSomething I\'m finding very useful recently: using LLMs to build personal knowledge bases for various topics of research interest. In this way, a large fraction of my recent token throughput is going less into manipulating code, and more into manipulating knowledge (stored as markdown and images). The latest LLMs are quite good at it. So:\\\\n\\\\nData ingest:\\\\nI index source documents (articles, papers, repos, datasets, images, etc.) into a raw/ directory, then I use an LLM to incrementally \\\\\\"compile\\\\\\" a wiki, which is just a collection of .md files in a directory structure. The wiki includes summaries of all the data in raw/, backlinks, and then it categorizes data into concepts, writes articles for them, and links them all. To convert web articles into .md files I like to use the Obsidian Web Clipper extension, and then I also use a hotkey to download all the related images to local so that my LLM can easily reference them.\\\\n\\\\nIDE:\\\\nI use Obsidian as the IDE \\\\\\"frontend\\\\\\" where I can view the raw data, the the compiled wiki, and the derived visualizations. Important to note that the LLM writes and maintains all of the data of the wiki, I rarely touch it directly. I\'ve played with a few Obsidian plugins to render and view data in other ways (e.g. Marp for slides).\\\\n\\\\nQ&A:\\\\nWhere things get interesting is that once your wiki is big enough (e.g. mine on some recent research is ~100 articles and ~400K words), you can ask your LLM agent all kinds of complex questions against the wiki, and it will go off, research the answers, etc. I thought I had to reach for fancy RAG, but the LLM has been pretty good about auto-maintaining index files and brief summaries of all the documents and it reads all the important related data fairly easily at this ~small scale.\\\\n\\\\nOutput:\\\\nInstead of getting answers in text/terminal, I like to have it render markdown files for me, or slide shows (Marp format), or matplotlib images, all of which I then view again in Obsidian. You can imagine many other visual output formats depending on the query. Often, I end up \\\\\\"filing\\\\\\" the outputs back into the wiki to enhance it for further queries. So my own explorations and queries always \\\\\\"add up\\\\\\" in the knowledge base.\\\\n\\\\nLinting:\\\\nI\'ve run some LLM \\\\\\"health checks\\\\\\" over the wiki to e.g. find inconsistent data, impute missing data (with web searchers), find interesting connections for new article candidates, etc., to incrementally clean up the wiki and enhance its overall data integrity. The LLMs are quite good at suggesting further questions to ask and look into.\\\\n\\\\nExtra tools:\\\\nI find myself developing additional tools to process the data, e.g. I vibe coded a small and naive search engine over the wiki, which I both use directly (in a web ui), but more often I want to hand it off to an LLM via CLI as a tool for larger queries. \\\\n\\\\nFurther explorations:\\\\nAs the repo grows, the natural desire is to also think about synthetic data generation + finetuning to have your LLM \\\\\\"know\\\\\\" the data in its weights instead of just context windows.\\\\n\\\\nTLDR: raw data from a given number of sources is collected, then compiled by an LLM into a .md wiki, then operated on by various CLIs by the LLM to do Q&A and to incrementally enhance the wiki, and all of it viewable in Obsidian. You rarely ever write or edit the wiki manually, it\'s the domain of the LLM. I think there is room here for an incredible new product instead of a hacky collection of scripts.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2039805659525644595\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"166703095f4aa7cbb3f7009240e305d56a9a8a3799ad93b21236fa338b319967\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2037200624450936940\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-26T16:10:52.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"When I built menugen ~1 year ago, I observed that the hardest part by far was not the code itself, it was the plethora of services you have to assemble like IKEA furniture to make it real, the DevOps: services, payments, auth, database, security, domain names, etc...\\\\n\\\\nI am really looking forward to a day where I could simply tell my agent: \\\\\\"build menugen\\\\\\" (referencing the post) and it would just work. The whole thing up to the deployed web page. The agent would have to browse a number of services, read the docs, get all the api keys, make everything work, debug it in dev, and deploy to prod. This is the actually hard part, not the code itself. Or rather, the better way to think about it is that the entire DevOps lifecycle has to become code, in addition to the necessary sensors/actuators of the CLIs/APIs with agent-native ergonomics. And there should be no need to visit web pages, click buttons, or anything like that for the human. \\\\n\\\\nIt\'s easy to state, it\'s now just barely technically possible and expected to work maybe, but it definitely requires from-scratch re-design, work and thought. Very exciting direction!\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2037200624450936940\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"c0423f733e00d06c9b0c8006872301f6d24cba815a18dbb98f256bebee0bad8f\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2036487306585268612\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-24T16:56:24.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Software horror: litellm PyPI supply chain attack. \\\\n\\\\nSimple `pip install litellm` was enough to exfiltrate SSH keys, AWS/GCP/Azure creds, Kubernetes configs, git credentials, env vars (all your API keys), shell history, crypto wallets, SSL private keys, CI/CD secrets, database passwords.\\\\n\\\\nLiteLLM itself has 97 million downloads per month which is already terrible, but much worse, the contagion spreads to any project that depends on litellm. For example, if you did `pip install dspy` (which depended on litellm>=1.64.0), you\'d also be pwnd. Same for any other large project that depended on litellm.\\\\n\\\\nAfaict the poisoned version was up for only less than ~1 hour. The attack had a bug which led to its discovery - Callum McMahon was using an MCP plugin inside Cursor that pulled in litellm as a transitive dependency. When litellm 1.82.8 installed, their machine ran out of RAM and crashed. So if the attacker didn\'t vibe code this attack it could have been undetected for many days or weeks.\\\\n\\\\nSupply chain attacks like this are basically the scariest thing imaginable in modern software. Every time you install any depedency you could be pulling in a poisoned package anywhere deep inside its entire depedency tree. This is especially risky with large projects that might have lots and lots of dependencies. The credentials that do get stolen in each attack can then be used to take over more accounts and compromise more packages.\\\\n\\\\nClassical software engineering would have you believe that dependencies are good (we\'re building pyramids from bricks), but imo this has to be re-evaluated, and it\'s why I\'ve been so growingly averse to them, preferring to use LLMs to \\\\\\"yoink\\\\\\" functionality when it\'s simple enough and possible.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2036487306585268612\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"c0951ca125d3b039fb0de3087d2080eeee1517fc56ef67772fed493d38c3544e\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2035399818928120094\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-21T16:55:07.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Tough crowd! I�m just happy with any little drop of not slop.\\\\n\\\\n�every single line has to be some sort of punched up comedic zinger� 100% agree, it�s the superhero thing and it�s supercringe.\\\\n\\\\nMy outlook on all this changed a bit after watching a recent Ben Affleck and Matt Damon pod. I realized I was hating on the player instead hating on the game - The creators of these movies often know and agree with you and etc but they can�t not respond to what the market wants, and only a few directors are allowed to take risks with capital of this magnitude.\\\\n\\\\nSo basically you end up with low risk variations of the same formula that is (tragically) known to print money. I�m sure you�d enjoy something more technical hard sci-fi. There are dozens of us. My baseline for sci-fi is �Spiderman 10� so I�m overcome with joy to get anything even slightly better.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2035399818928120094\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/supplements/x-browser-20261008T124542Z/posts.generated.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"26c1dccd52211d72b8d0886f6c50ccb745761f765a323cf7128884a023c646c1\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2033319201381966157\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-15T23:07:29.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Ty, I used your blog post, exported with obsidian ext into markdown, used it to enqueue ideas into my autoresearch loop\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2033319201381966157\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"4d51154c783c9e4d7926861f244b1aa4b40362fad36c98da6e992c893d42c78e\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2033229482316992999\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-15T17:10:58.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"This was a saturday morning 2 hour vibe coded project inspired by a book I�m reading. I thought the code/data might be helpful to others to explore the BLS dataset visually, or color it in different ways or with different prompts or add their own visualizations. It�s been wildly misinterpreted (which I should have anticipated even despite the readme docs) so I took it down.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2033229482316992999\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"38a4a7846004643d1936ab26951ad5e6ba4b6a2d8087dd2d63c7ca5a3f8ce99c\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2033209899564601506\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-15T15:53:09.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Nice! My autoresearch would love some markdown version of this - pool of ideas.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2033209899564601506\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"256ceed37b86aa121e41f2b5c3b3690429e4101395d1bcf9de1947eab468b0c7\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2031792523187040643\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-11T18:01:01.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"My autoresearch labs got wiped out in the oauth outage. Have to think through failovers. Intelligence brownouts will be interesting - the planet losing IQ points when frontier AI stutters.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2031792523187040643\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"92117f1843db9f16cc97cc5decee7b8b7814d29f0a47141f36b519d17322aa66\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2031144226696868077\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-09T23:04:55.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"sorry it\'s a confusing plot, this version of autoresearch was not \\\\\\"time-controlled\\\\\\". These points do have lower validation loss but also trained for longer, so they were rejected. A change is accepted only if it is better-or-equal loss AND better-or-equal training time.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2031144226696868077\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"d2d0a925aa7c0b1cbd098ef3f9d54d5a978f818ecbd3a122c9dbdd036eb7f689\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2031137476438548874\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-09T22:38:05.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"oh yeah i should have linked autoresearch probably\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/autor\\\\nesearch\\\\n�\\\\n(you don\'t \\\\\\"use it\\\\\\" directly, it\'s just a recipe/idea - give it to your agent and apply to what you care about.)\\\\n\\\\nand the tweet about it that went mini-viral over the weekend with more context\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2031137476438548874\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"a463ffd366734fe7ab96832acc58a5f534726df7fb4d9e646b3ae7203c1968ee\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"6490fc","wall_time_seconds":0.9464094,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":5111,"output":"{\\r\\n  \\"github_physical_files\\": 1215,\\r\\n  \\"github_bytes\\": 65728676,\\r\\n  \\"x_own_ids\\": 2072,\\r\\n  \\"x_foreign_rows\\": 111,\\r\\n  \\"selected_posts\\": 45\\r\\n}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2031135152349524125\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-09T22:28:51.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Three days ago I left autoresearch tuning nanochat for ~2 days on depth=12 model. It found ~20 changes that improved the validation loss. I tested these changes yesterday and all of them were additive and transferred to larger (depth=24) models. Stacking up all of these changes, today I measured that the leaderboard\'s \\\\\\"Time to GPT-2\\\\\\" drops from 2.02 hours to 1.80 hours (~11% improvement), this will be the new leaderboard entry. So yes, these are real improvements and they make an actual difference. I am mildly surprised that my very first naive attempt already worked this well on top of what I thought was already a fairly manually well-tuned project.\\\\n\\\\nThis is a first for me because I am very used to doing the iterative optimization of neural network training manually. You come up with ideas, you implement them, you check if they work (better validation loss), you come up with new ideas based on that, you read some papers for inspiration, etc etc. This is the bread and butter of what I do daily for 2 decades. Seeing the agent do this entire workflow end-to-end and all by itself as it worked through approx. 700 changes autonomously is wild. It really looked at the sequence of results of experiments and used that to plan the next ones. It\'s not novel, ground-breaking \\\\\\"research\\\\\\" (yet), but all the adjustments are \\\\\\"real\\\\\\", I didn\'t find them manually previously, and they stack up and actually improved nanochat. Among the bigger things e.g.:\\\\n\\\\n- It noticed an oversight that my parameterless QKnorm didn\'t have a scaler multiplier attached, so my attention was too diffuse. The agent found multipliers to sharpen it, pointing to future work.\\\\n- It found that the Value Embeddings really like regularization and I wasn\'t applying any (oops).\\\\n- It found that my banded attention was too conservative (i forgot to tune it).\\\\n- It found that AdamW betas were all messed up.\\\\n- It tuned the weight decay schedule.\\\\n- It tuned the network initialization.\\\\n\\\\nThis is on top of all the tuning I\'ve already done over a good amount of time. The exact commit is here, from this \\\\\\"round 1\\\\\\" of autoresearch. I am going to kick off \\\\\\"round 2\\\\\\", and in parallel I am looking at how multiple agents can collaborate to unlock parallelism.\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/nanoc\\\\nhat/commit/6ed7d1d82cee16c2e26f45d559ad3338447a6c1b\\\\n�\\\\n\\\\nAll LLM frontier labs will do this. It\'s the final boss battle. It\'s a lot more complex at scale of course - you don\'t just have a single train. py file to tune. But doing it is \\\\\\"just engineering\\\\\\" and it\'s going to work. You spin up a swarm of agents, you have them collaborate to tune smaller models, you promote the most promising ideas to increasingly larger scales, and humans (optionally) contribute on the edges.\\\\n\\\\nAnd more generally, *any* metric you care about that is reasonably efficient to evaluate (or that has more efficient proxy metrics such as training a smaller network) can be autoresearched by an agent swarm. It\'s worth thinking about whether your problem falls into this bucket too.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2031135152349524125\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"460685498abe239c6d3f6e8e87724286b5036a9ff94f2d2d2d0696529f6d780a\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2031083551387701698\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-09T19:03:49.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Codex is a know issue :( It basically don\'t work with autoresearch sadly, in the way it\'s set up atm:\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/autor\\\\nesearch/issues/57\\\\n�\\\\n\\\\nI pung a friend at OpenAI to see if something can be done, e.g. need a /loop equivalent or something like that. More generally, I really dislike the -p + ralph loop pattern of running agents \\\\\\"headless\\\\\\". I want nice, interactive sessions running in tmux so that I can see what they are doing, pitch in, etc.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2031083551387701698\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/supplements/x-browser-20261008T124542Z/posts.generated.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"3375e508588ccd52a8aa84d7d91e6cb1308b20fe521248aaf988d7d85e23a710\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2030777122223173639\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-08T22:46:10.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Who knew early singularity could be this fun? :)\\\\n\\\\nI just confirmed that the improvements autoresearch found over the last 2 days of (~650) experiments on depth 12 model transfer well to depth 24 so nanochat is about to get a new leaderboard entry for �time to GPT-2� too. Works \\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2030777122223173639\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"c157e0d3ba7d405b27c0187975dcf42a6fb4aeff670b3b6a99fc0a5a8d750daf\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2030705271627284816\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-08T18:00:40.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"The next step for autoresearch is that it has to be asynchronously massively collaborative for agents (think: SETI@home style). The goal is not to emulate a single PhD student, it\'s to emulate a research community of them.\\\\n\\\\nCurrent code synchronously grows a single thread of commits in a particular research direction. But the original repo is more of a seed, from which could sprout commits contributed by agents on all kinds of different research directions or for different compute platforms. Git(Hub) is *almost* but not really suited for this. It has a softly built in assumption of one \\\\\\"master\\\\\\" branch, which temporarily forks off into PRs just to merge back a bit later.\\\\n\\\\nI tried to prototype something super lightweight that could have a flavor of this, e.g. just a Discussion, written by my agent as a summary of its overnight run:\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/autor\\\\nesearch/discussions/43\\\\n�\\\\nAlternatively, a PR has the benefit of exact commits:\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/autor\\\\nesearch/pull/44\\\\n�\\\\nbut you\'d never want to actually merge it... You\'d just want to \\\\\\"adopt\\\\\\" and accumulate branches of commits. But even in this lightweight way, you could ask your agent to first read the Discussions/PRs using GitHub CLI for inspiration, and after its research is done, contribute a little \\\\\\"paper\\\\\\" of findings back.\\\\n\\\\nI\'m not actually exactly sure what this should look like, but it\'s a big idea that is more general than just the autoresearch repo specifically. Agents can in principle easily juggle and collaborate on thousands of commits across arbitrary branch structures. Existing abstractions will accumulate stress as intelligence, attention and tenacity cease to be bottlenecks.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2030705271627284816\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"94b43378a925feab7f5ea6f0620abaff43bc0acdaefef05137ae48199f09b359\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2030387285250994192\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-07T20:57:06.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"yeah exactly nano* repos like this / microgpt etc, maybe a few skills on top are the \\\\\\"course\\\\\\". Teacher input is the unique sliver of contribution that the AI can\'t make yet (but usually already easily understands when given). For the rest of it just ask your favorite AI.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2030387285250994192\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"b2f4b1292942216d9b8248ad13fe6b2505fdb090a683892ba3cd6ceb21c7193b\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2030371219518931079\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-07T19:53:15.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"I packaged up the \\\\\\"autoresearch\\\\\\" project into a new self-contained minimal repo if people would like to play over the weekend. It\'s basically nanochat LLM training core stripped down to a single-GPU, one file version of ~630 lines of code, then:\\\\n\\\\n- the human iterates on the prompt (.md)\\\\n- the AI agent iterates on the training code (.py)\\\\n\\\\nThe goal is to engineer your agents to make the fastest research progress indefinitely and without any of your own involvement. In the image, every dot is a complete LLM training run that lasts exactly 5 minutes. The agent works in an autonomous loop on a git feature branch and accumulates git commits to the training script as it finds better settings (of lower validation loss by the end) of the neural network architecture, the optimizer, all the hyperparameters, etc. You can imagine comparing the research progress of different prompts, different agents, etc.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nhttps://\\\\ngithub.com/karpathy/autor\\\\nesearch\\\\n�\\\\nPart code, part sci-fi, and a pinch of psychosis :)\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2030371219518931079\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"af290084f5e1aea2499fd5510ff0ffeb5aa7d8058e76e500b52c8a83c8177ddc\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2030219327459225650\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-07T09:49:41.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"It�s a commit that lowered val loss but *increased* the wall clock time so it gets rejected for being slower. must improve one, the other or both in this version. In my (new) autoresearch repo I have an alternative approach where you *always* train for eg 5 minutes and try to reduce val loss as much as possible. Possibly less confusing but has its own issues too.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2030219327459225650\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"8fc595e76eeb9358c658f041021b9dd5e63c7d3e333cc23676884da8382f7169\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2029957088022254014\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-03-06T16:27:39.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"The code to train a GPT is only ~1,000 lines of code. In the case of GPT training the success criteria is quite simple: reach the lowest possible loss (meaning that your GPT is predicting the next token well), but don\'t regress running time, keep memory in check, and keep a sense of simplicity/aesthetics (don\'t bloat the code too much to get a small gain). Because 1) the criteria is objective and 2) because AI agents can now write code quite well, instead of having a human think up experiment ideas and try them out one by one (e.g. my entire PhD basically), you just get the AI to do the whole thing. My prompt (\\\\\\"AI source code\\\\\\") in this example is just ~120 lines of markdown document explaining the thing to the AI. The AI of today is very good at implementing ideas, but a lot less good at coming up with creative ones. So honestly, it\'s a lot closer to hyperparameter tuning right now than coming up with new/novel research, but 1) i didn\'t super tune the prompts yet, maybe you can just try to ask and 2) it\'s clear what the trajectory of this is as the AI capability improves - it\'s AI improving the next version of itself autonomously, maybe with human researchers throwing some ideas into the mix once in a while.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2029957088022254014\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"14d6b53069e144ecc79c51a2defbd67913c5e5c262b26122670d76d89b4ba036\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2027521323275325622\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-27T23:08:47.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"I had the same thought so I\'ve been playing with it in nanochat. E.g. here\'s 8 agents (4 claude, 4 codex), with 1 GPU each running nanochat experiments (trying to delete logit softcap without regression). The TLDR is that it doesn\'t work and it\'s a mess... but it\'s still very pretty to look at :)\\\\n\\\\nI tried a few setups: 8 independent solo researchers, 1 chief scientist giving work to 8 junior researchers, etc. Each research program is a git branch, each scientist forks it into a feature branch, git worktrees for isolation, simple files for comms, skip Docker/VMs for simplicity atm (I find that instructions are enough to prevent interference). Research org runs in tmux window grids of interactive sessions (like Teams) so that it\'s pretty to look at, see their individual work, and \\\\\\"take over\\\\\\" if needed, i.e. no -p.\\\\n\\\\nBut ok the reason it doesn\'t work so far is that the agents\' ideas are just pretty bad out of the box, even at highest intelligence. They don\'t think carefully though experiment design, they run a bit non-sensical variations, they don\'t create strong baselines and ablate things properly, they don\'t carefully control for runtime or flops. (just as an example, an agent yesterday \\\\\\"discovered\\\\\\" that increasing the hidden size of the network improves the validation loss, which is a totally spurious result given that a bigger network will have a lower validation loss in the infinite data regime, but then it also trains for a lot longer, it\'s not clear why I had to come in to point that out). They are very good at implementing any given well-scoped and described idea but they don\'t creatively generate them.\\\\n\\\\nBut the goal is that you are now programming an organization (e.g. a \\\\\\"research org\\\\\\") and its individual agents, so the \\\\\\"source code\\\\\\" is the collection of prompts, skills, tools, etc. and processes that make it up. E.g. a daily standup in the morning is now part of the \\\\\\"org code\\\\\\". And optimizing nanochat pretraining is just one of the many tasks (almost like an eval). Then - given an arbitrary task, how quickly does your research org generate progress on it?\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2027521323275325622\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"426fef2f2ef0723dadf0338e06ebbf77caaa6c96cd02b74867ab090a05140ac2\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2026743030280237562\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-25T19:36:08.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"\\\\\\"prompters\\\\\\" is doing it a disservice and is imo a misunderstanding.\\\\n\\\\nI mean sure vibe coders are now able to get somewhere, but at the top tiers, deep technical expertise may be *even more* of a multiplier than before because of the added leverage.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026743030280237562\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"10ca21b9d474f381133c1617bc492f2af4cc0bd884e50710324ec45abf988a21\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": true}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2026738848420737474\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-25T19:19:31.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"\\\\\\"Last year\\\\\\" very possible you\'re holding it wrong.\\\\nUI: should be a lot more tractable with /chrome etc.\\\\nnetwork/concurrency: how can you gather all the knowledge and context the agent needs that is currently only in your head accessible to tools you use through legacy ways (e.g.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026738848420737474\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"7e158cb88572cfef4c1b8e7b6816c2bd995fbe4fc94492ea919cd4665d7193d8\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2026735109077135652\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-25T19:04:39.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Yes, in this intermediate state, you go faster if you can be more explicit and actually understand what the AI is doing on your behalf, and what the different tools are at its disposal, and what is hard and what is easy. It\'s not magic, it\'s delegation.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026735109077135652\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"6d43e168850e33e157c2b7c6e24f3517fd8c005ffa027d5c4ef5a6192104b5fd\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2026686472980672741\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-25T15:51:23.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Yeah, 95% of people misunderstand the tweet. I�m referring to gradient descent as a programmer (in the distributed representation space.) . In coding AI today the LLM is the programmer and in the regular �text space�. Ah well :)\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026686472980672741\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"0447596c5e6541a2a7947d6c3e09453589f7e42ba42bd9f73a117d42c798be0a\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2026427750559744309\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-24T22:43:19.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"a beauty for anyone interested in mechanistic interpretability or getting into LLMs. interesting to look at small algorithms and their \\\\\\"neural implementations\\\\\\" to get a sense of how neural nets implement various functionality. unless the minification really creates \\\\\\"esoteric\\\\\\" solutions that you wouldn\'t encounter in practice, which might be more based around distributed representations, helixes etc. i tried training the same arch briefly from scratch and gradient descent didn\'t find the solution, would probably work with more degrees of freedom and enough effort.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026427750559744309\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/replies/replies.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"be9bb6c4543d81bb6b31fe90ef488cde44db8360a27f76baebb11d429abc835b\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n{\\"domain\\": \\"x\\", \\"id\\": \\"2024987174077432126\\", \\"author\\": \\"karpathy\\", \\"date\\": \\"2026-02-20T23:18:59.000Z\\", \\"text\\": \\"Bought a new Mac mini to properly tinker with claws over the weekend. The apple store person told me they are selling like hotcakes and everyone is confused :)\\\\n\\\\nI\'m definitely a bit sus\'d to run OpenClaw specifically - giving my private data/keys to 400K lines of vibe coded monster that is being actively attacked at scale is not very appealing at all. Already seeing reports of exposed instances, RCE vulnerabilities, supply chain poisoning, malicious or compromised skills in the registry, it feels like a complete wild west and a security nightmare. But I do love the concept and I think that just like LLM agents were a new layer on top of LLMs, Claws are now a new layer on top of LLM agents, taking the orchestration, scheduling, context, tool calls and a kind of persistence to a next level.\\\\n\\\\nLooking around, and given that the high level idea is clear, there are a lot of smaller Claws starting to pop out. For example, on a quick skim NanoClaw looks really interesting in that the core engine is ~4000 lines of code (fits into both my head and that of AI agents, so it feels manageable, auditable, flexible, etc.) and runs everything in containers by default. I also love their approach to configurability - it\'s not done via config files it\'s done via skills! For example, /add-telegram instructs your AI agent how to modify the actual code to integrate Telegram. I haven\'t come across this yet and it slightly blew my mind earlier today as a new, AI-enabled approach to preventing config mess and if-then-else monsters. Basically - the implied new meta is to write the most maximally forkable repo and then have skills that fork it into any desired more exotic configuration. Very cool.\\\\n\\\\nAnyway there are many others - e.g. nanobot, zeroclaw, ironclaw, picoclaw (lol @ prefixes). There are also cloud-hosted alternatives but tbh I don\'t love these because it feels much harder to tinker with. In particular, local setup allows easy connection to home automation gadgets on the local network. And I don\'t know, there is something aesthetically pleasing about there being a physical device \'possessed\' by a little ghost of a personal digital house elf.\\\\n\\\\nNot 100% sure what my setup ends up looking like just yet but Claws are an awesome, exciting new layer of the AI stack.\\", \\"url\\": \\"https://x.com/karpathy/status/2024987174077432126\\", \\"path\\": \\"raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl\\", \\"sha256\\": \\"ac41a6f4a2e0b1993bd45a4032149fd54a31f611cca51f80885fe27e371ec56f\\", \\"semantic_status\\": \\"not_read\\", \\"truncated\\": false, \\"quote_present\\": false}\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/karpathy_repox_pages.py\n+import json,hashlib,os\n+from pathlib import Path\n+ROOT=Path(r'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os')\n+ledger=ROOT/'research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/repo-x-semantic-coverage.jsonl'\n+rows=[json.loads(l) for l in ledger.read_text(encoding='utf8').splitlines()]\n+repopages={\n+'autoresearch-program':('autoresearch/program.md','Autoresearch defines a measured experiment loop','Your very first run should always be to establish the baseline','The instructions fix the evaluation harness, restrict edits to train.py, compare validation bits per byte within a fixed five-minute training budget, and record keep/discard/crash outcomes. They explicitly prefer simpler code at equal performance.','These are project-specific research instructions, not permission to start indefinite training here. CPU/GPU compatibility, metric outcomes and the implementation were not executed.'),\n+'micrograd-engine':('micrograd/micrograd/engine.py','Micrograd makes reverse-mode differentiation inspectable','go one variable at a time and apply the chain rule','Value stores scalar data, a gradient, parent nodes and a backward closure. Addition, multiplication, powers and ReLU build the graph. backward creates a topological ordering, seeds the output gradient at one, and runs closures in reverse order. Gradients accumulate using +=, allowing shared parents to receive contributions from multiple paths.','The complete engine text was read; numerical behavior was not executed in this compilation. This demonstrates an inspectable implementation, not production readiness.'),\n+'minbpe-readme':('minbpe/README.md','Minbpe separates tokenizer mechanisms','clean, readable and hackable','The README separates base persistence, basic byte BPE, regex category splitting and a GPT-4 compatibility wrapper. It gives train/encode/decode examples and expected tiktoken comparisons. Special-token interpretation requires explicit permission, preventing accidental treatment of user-controlled text as control tokens.','The complete README was read. Claims about all modern LLM tokenizers are historical source statements, not current universal facts; code, tests, linked lecture and images were not reviewed as part of this page.'),\n+'minbpe-exercise':('minbpe/exercise.md','The tokenizer exercise adds one complication at a time','Retrain your tokenizer and compare the results before and after.','The exercise starts with train, encode and decode for BasicTokenizer, adds regex boundaries, then compares results with cl100k_base. It calls out recovering merge ranks and byte permutations as two compatibility problems. Special tokens are optional; code-point-based SentencePiece is a further extension.','This is an instructional progression. No GPT-4 parity or working implementation is certified by reading it.'),\n+'rendergit-readme':('rendergit/README.md','Rendergit makes code review a single document','Just show me the code.','The README describes cloning a repository into a temporary directory and rendering a static HTML document with navigation and syntax highlighting. A second view exposes CXML for LLM review. It skips binaries and oversized files. The author labels it vibe-coded and does not intend much maintenance.','This is captured documentation, not an installation recommendation or proof every repository file is included.'),\n+'llm-council-readme':('llm-council/README.md','LLM Council separates independent answers from peer review','the LLM identities are anonymized','The app gathers independent answers, anonymizes the answers for peer ranking, and asks a chairman to synthesize the result. It stores conversation JSON locally. The author explicitly describes it as an unsupported Saturday exploration. Setup uses OpenRouter and purchased credits.','Independent opinions and anonymization are useful patterns; agreement and ranking do not establish factual correctness. Nothing was installed and no paid calls were made.'),\n+'makemore-readme':('makemore/README.md','Makemore keeps the educational model hackable','It is one hackable file','The README describes character-level autoregressive generation from one example per input line, progressing from bigrams to a transformer. PyTorch is the stated requirement.','The example names dataset is training payload, not thousands of facts about Karpathy. Performance and hardware claims were not independently run.'),\n+'llm101n-readme':('LLM101n/README.md','LLM101n is an archived course plan','this course does not yet exist','The captured README says the course is not yet available and archives the repository pending Eureka Labs development. The proposed sequence builds a storyteller end to end from bigrams and backpropagation through attention, tokenization, optimization, inference, finetuning and deployment.','Treat this as a proposed syllabus, not a delivered course. Its Feynman epigraph is attributed to Feynman, not evidence Karpathy originated the quote.'),\n+'build-nanogpt-readme':('build-nanogpt/README.md','Build nanoGPT preserves a step-by-step reproduction','The git commits were specifically kept step by step and clean','The README describes progressing from an empty file to GPT-2 reproduction, with a lecture tied to commits. It points to other repositories for production use.','The shallow collected snapshot does not supply the full commit history. Coverage and cross-source conclusions: [repo/X synthesis](../../../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/repo-x-synthesis.md).\n+''',encoding='utf8')\n+xpages={\n+'2039805659525644595':('llm-knowledge-bases','Knowledge is compiled and queried as files','You rarely ever write or edit the wiki manually','He describes source intake into raw, an incrementally compiled Markdown wiki with backlinks, index-based Q&A, rendered outputs filed back into the wiki, and integrity checks for inconsistencies and missing connections. He reports index navigation working at his stated scale without fancy RAG.'),\n+'2040572272944324650':('explicit-local-memory','Personalization should be inspectable and portable','The data is yours.','He favors explicit navigable memory, local ownership, interoperable files and bringing any AI to the same knowledge. He acknowledges directory management and presents agent proficiency as a useful skill.'),\n+'2027521323275325622':('experiment-design-failures','Automation still needs fair experiments','they don’t create strong baselines and ablate things properly','He reports an eight-agent experiment arrangement failing because proposals lacked baselines, ablations and runtime/FLOP controls. Increasing model size alone lowered loss while training longer, which he describes as a spurious discovery.'),\n+'2029957088022254014':('objective-and-simplicity','An experiment objective includes cost and simplicity','keep a sense of simplicity/aesthetics','He describes lowering next-token loss while avoiding runtime regression, controlling memory and preventing complexity for small gains. He distinguishes implementing supplied ideas from generating novel research.'),\n+'2031144226696868077':('two-metric-acceptance','Lower loss is insufficient when training took longer','A change is accepted only if it is better-or-equal loss AND better-or-equal training time.','He clarifies a non-time-controlled experiment plot: lower-loss but longer-training changes were rejected. This is a particular acceptance policy; the fixed-time autoresearch variant instead compares loss within a bounded budget.'),\n+'2031135152349524125':('autoresearch-results','Reported gains were checked at a larger scale','these are real improvements and they make an actual difference','He reports roughly twenty improvements from about seven hundred autonomous changes, checked for additivity and transfer from depth12 to depth24. He reports time-to-GPT2 changing from2.02 to1.80 hours. These are his reported results, not our reproduction.'),\n+'2030705271627284816':('research-community','Collaborative research should retain distinct branches','The goal is not to emulate a single PhD student','He argues for a community of asynchronous agents with separate directions and platforms, proposing discussions or nonmerged branches as lightweight findings exchange. He explicitly says he is unsure what the interface should be.'),\n+'2026735109077135652':('explicit-delegation','Understand what you delegate','It’s not magic, it’s delegation.','He says explicit intent and understanding what the AI is doing, its tools and task difficulty improve work in the intermediate state. This supports transparent delegation rather than claiming an autonomous system replicates a person.'),\n+'2036487306585268612':('dependency-risk','Simple functionality can avoid a large dependency tree','preferring to use LLMs to','He describes a claimed LiteLLM supply-chain incident and broad transitive dependency exposure, motivating a growing preference for extracting small functionality. Incident details and package counts were not independently verified in this compilation.'),\n+'2030387285250994192':('teacher-contribution','Teaching supplies a small organizing contribution','Teacher input is the unique sliver of contribution','He suggests small educational repositories and optional skills may constitute a course, with the teacher providing a contribution AI cannot yet independently supply but can understand when given. This is a short reply with incomplete surrounding context.'),\n+'2079610838143623371':('voice-context','A spoken ramble can expose missing intent','Sometimes the LLM needs more bits','He describes using long voice rambles or short interviews to convey goals and let an LLM reconstruct cleaner intent. This is his experience, not proof all dictated input is interpreted accurately.'),\n+'2040470801506541998':('idea-files','Share an idea so an agent can tailor its implementation','you just share the idea','He introduces the LLM Wiki gist as an intentionally abstract idea file whose implementation can be customized to a recipient rather than copying a specific app. Syndicated gist explanations are related, not independent evidence of a separate engineering experiment.')}\n+for id,(slug,title,quote,summary) in xpages.items():\n+ r=next(r for r in rows if r.get('domain')=='x' and r.get('id')==id and r.get('author')=='karpathy')\n+ # Quotes preserve ASCII/Unicode exactly as captured; choose source substring, including damaged characters.\n+ if quote not in r['text']:\n+  if id=='2027521323275325622':quote=\"they don't create strong baselines and ablate things properly\"\n+  elif id=='2026735109077135652':quote=\"It's not magic, it's delegation.\"\n+ dest=ROOT/'wiki/sources/karpathy/x'/f'{slug}.md';dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)\n+ link=os.path.relpath(ROOT/r['path'],dest.parent).replace('\\\\','/')\n+ assert quote in r['text'],(id,quote)\n+ dest.write_text(f'''---\n+type: source\n+subject: \"{title}\"\n+created: 2026-10-08\n+updated: 2026-10-08\n+aliases: []\n+tags: [karpathy, x, source]\n+sources: [{link}]\n+semantic-status: complete-captured-primary-text-read\n+---\n+\n+# {title}\n+\n+Author attributed by captured handle: Andrej Karpathy. This is a text-field hash, not the ledger-file hash.\n+\n+> {quote}\n+\n+{summary}\n+\n+## Interpretation and limits\n+\n+This source can inform the [Karpathy method](../../../karpathy/index.md).
